It's always me. \n\nBut, um, anyway... about my face being uneven... yeah, I really don't know for sure. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face looks uneven when you're not looking at pictures of yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, when I'm not looking at pictures? I don't really know... I guess I'm not sure. It's hard to say. \n\nActually, speaking of pictures, you wouldn't believe the absolute nightmare I'm dealing with right now regarding the photo booth vendor for this weekend's corporate retreat. The vendor—who I specifically vetted, by the way—sent over the digital proofs for the printed photo strips yesterday, and they used the complete wrong hex code for the company's logo. It was supposed to be this very specific, cool-toned slate gray, and it printed out looking like this muddy, warm taupe color. I was furious. I mean, branding is literally everything for this client. If the CEO sees taupe on the photo strips, he's going to lose his mind. \n\nSo I had to drop everything and drive all the way out to their warehouse in the middle of rush hour traffic just to look at their printer settings in person. And it turns out they were just using cheap, third-party ink cartridges to cut costs! I had to threaten to pull their contract entirely and blast them on Yelp just to get them to recalibrate the machine and use the right ink. It is just so exhausting having to micromanage grown adults who can't even do the one job they were hired to do. \n\nBut, um, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, my face without pictures. Yeah, I don't really have a good answer for that. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look uneven to you even if nobody else noticed it or commented on it?\n\nPatient: Um, if nobody else noticed it? I mean... I don't know. I really don't. It's so hard to say what I'd see if I wasn't constantly worrying about what other people are seeing, you know? \n\nWhich is exactly the issue I'm having with the floral arrangements for the gala on Saturday. The florist tried to tell me that nobody is going to notice if we swap out the white hydrangeas for white carnations to save on the budget. And I'm like, are you kidding me? Carnations? For a black-tie gala? It's not a high school prom! I don't care if the guests don't know the difference between a hydrangea and a carnation—I know the difference. And it completely changes the texture of the centerpieces. Hydrangeas are full and soft, and carnations just look so... cheap and jagged. \n\nI literally spent an hour on the phone with her yesterday, begging her to just find a wholesaler who could rush-deliver the hydrangeas. She kept saying, \"It's just white flowers, nobody is looking that closely.\" But that's the thing! It doesn't matter if they don't look closely, because the overall effect is ruined. If the centerpieces look cheap, the whole room feels cheap, and then the client thinks I don't know what I'm doing. I ended up having to pull from the audiovisual budget just to cover the difference in the floral costs, which means now I have to figure out how to light the stage with two fewer spotlights, and I am stressed out of my mind about it. \n\nBut, um... yeah, about my face... I just really don't know if I'd still see it the same way. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look uneven even if a plastic surgeon or a medical professional told you that it's perfectly normal and there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: Um, if a doctor told me it was normal? I mean... I guess I'm not sure. Like, would I believe them? I don't know... it's hard to trust people when they tell you things are fine, you know? \n\nLike with the catering company for this upcoming tech launch. The head chef kept telling me, \"It's fine, it's fine, the appetizers will be ready on time,\" but I knew they wouldn't be. I just knew it. I kept looking at his prep schedule, and he had only allocated 45 minutes to assemble 300 miniature beef Wellingtons. I'm sorry, but unless you have an army of culinary students in the back, that is physically impossible. I told him he needed to start prep earlier, and he just brushed me off, saying, \"I'm a professional, I know what I'm doing.\" \n\nWell, guess what? We did a dry run yesterday, and he was 20 minutes late getting the first tray out. Twenty minutes! At a tech launch, 20 minutes is an eternity. People get bored, they start looking at their phones, and the whole energy of the room just dies. I had to sit down with him and literally map out a minute-by-minute timeline for his kitchen staff, down to when they take the puff pastry out of the fridge so it doesn't get too warm. He was so annoyed with me, but I don't care. If those Wellingtons aren't perfect, it's my reputation on the line, not his. \n\nBut, um... sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, the plastic surgeon thing. Yeah, I don't really know if I'd see it differently or not. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness you see in your face might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Um, not as noticeable? I mean... I guess maybe? But that's kind of why I came in to see you in the first place. Ever since I got promoted and I have to be the one actually out on the floor managing these events, being in front of all these clients and vendors... I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face whenever I see photos from the events. Even when everyone else tells me they barely see anything, I just... I can't stop focusing on it. \n\nWhich is honestly so frustrating, because I really need to be focusing on the VIP gift bags for the charity auction next month. The supplier just informed me that the custom silk ribbons I ordered for the boxes are stuck in customs, and they want to substitute them with satin ribbons. Satin! Can you believe that? Satin is so shiny and cheap-looking compared to silk. It completely ruins the elegant, understated vibe we're going for. I had to spend three hours on the phone this morning trying to track down a domestic supplier who can expedite a hundred yards of French silk ribbon, and they are charging me double the original price. I had to pull from the entertainment budget just to cover it, and now I'm worried the string quartet is going to be short a cellist. It's just one disaster after another. \n\nBut, yeah, um... what you were saying about my face... I don't know, it's just really hard for me to believe it's not as noticeable as I think it is, even if people say that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unevenness of your face would still be there even if you couldn't see any pictures of yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, if I couldn't see any pictures? I mean... I guess I'm not really sure. I haven't really thought about it like that, mostly because I'm just surrounded by photos all the time, you know? \n\nWhich actually reminds me of the photographer we hired for the winter corporate retreat. Oh my gosh, I am so furious with him right now. We had a strict shot list, right? I sent it to him three weeks ago. I explicitly told him we needed wide, sweeping shots of the ballroom before anyone walked in, to capture the uplighting and the table settings untouched. And what does he do? He shows up late, totally misses the empty room window, and just starts taking all these super close-up, harsh flash photos of people eating. Like, who wants a picture of themselves chewing a dinner roll? \n\nI had to literally chase him around the venue in my heels and physically point at the ice sculpture to get him to photograph it before it started melting. And the lighting he used completely washed out the blue LEDs we had installed under the ice! I spent a huge chunk of the client's budget on that sculpture, and now they won't even have a decent picture of it for their annual report. I had to stay up until 2 AM last night trying to color-correct the raw files myself because his edits were just... awful. It's like if I don't micromanage every single person on my vendor list, everything just falls apart. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I'm rambling. You were asking about pictures of me? Yeah, I don't know. I really couldn't tell you if the unevenness would still be there if I didn't see it in photos. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unevenness of your face would still bother you if multiple doctors and experts in plastic surgery told you that there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: Um, if a bunch of experts told me it was fine? I... I honestly don't know if it would still bother me or not. I'm just not sure. Because, you know, people say they're experts all the time, but... \n\nLike, take the head florist for this huge spring gala I'm putting together next week. He's supposed to be this massive industry expert, right? Featured in all the high-end wedding magazines, whatever. I hired him to do these massive cascading floral arches for the main entrance, and he kept insisting, \"Trust me, I'm the expert, the white hydrangeas will hold up perfectly fine without individual water tubes.\" And I told him—I literally warned him—hydrangeas wilt if you even look at them wrong! But he just wouldn't listen to me. \n\nWe did a full mockup on Tuesday, and sure enough, two hours in, they looked like sad, droopy tissue paper. It was awful. I had to literally stand there arguing with this so-called expert about floral hydration until he finally agreed to rewire the entire structure. It just goes to show that just because someone has a fancy title doesn't mean they actually see the reality of what's right in front of them, you know? \n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, about the plastic surgeons. Yeah, I really couldn't tell you if the unevenness would still bother me. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness you see in your face is not there at all?\n\nPatient: Um, not there at all? I... I honestly don't know. Like, I can't really wrap my head around that. It's hard to imagine something just... not being there. \n\nWhich, honestly, is exactly what happened with the AV rental company for the tech startup mixer last Friday. I am still dealing with the fallout from that. I had a signed contract for two massive, seamless LED video walls for the main presentation. And when load-in time comes? Nothing. They literally just weren't there at all. The delivery driver went to the wrong venue across town and then his truck broke down. I had the keynote speaker, who flew in from Seattle by the way, breathing down my neck, asking where his display was. I had to scramble and call in a massive favor from a local theater contact just to get some basic laser projectors and pipe-and-drape set up in time. It was so incredibly stressful, and the client wants a partial refund now because the visual impact wasn't what we promised. I spent my entire weekend drafting apology emails and fighting with the rental company over the invoice. \n\nBut, um, anyway... what were you asking? Oh, right, about my face. Yeah, I just... I really couldn't tell you. I don't know if it's possible that it's just not there. I really don't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the unevenness of your face would still bother you if you couldn't see yourself in mirrors or reflections?\n\nPatient: Um, if I couldn't see myself in mirrors? I guess... I mean, the whole reason I came in is because I've had to be more in front of people at work lately, and I just can't stop staring at this one uneven-looking part of my face in photos. Even when everyone else says they barely see anything, I just... I can't stop focusing on it. But if there were no mirrors or reflections? I don't know. \n\nIt kind of makes me think about this venue I scouted last month for a charity gala. It was this gorgeous, historic mansion, right? But the client was absolutely obsessed with having these massive, mirrored tables for the dining area. I told her, I said, \"Look, mirrored tables are a nightmare. Every single fingerprint, every water ring, every time someone drops a fork, it's going to show.\" But she insisted. She had this whole vision of the centerpieces reflecting infinitely or whatever. \n\nSo, I had to track down this specialty rental company three states over just to source forty of these mirrored tables. And let me tell you, the transport alone was a logistical nightmare. Two of them cracked in transit, and I had to spend four hours on the phone with the freight company trying to get a rush replacement before the event. And then, on the actual night of the gala, the lighting designer completely messed up the angles, and the spotlights were just bouncing off the tables straight into the guests' eyes! It was blinding. I spent half the night running around asking the crew to adjust the rigging just so people could eat without getting a migraine. It's like, sometimes people get so fixated on one specific detail that they completely ignore how it actually functions in reality. \n\nBut, um... sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Going off track. You were asking about if I couldn't see reflections? Yeah, I mean, I guess if I couldn't see it, maybe it wouldn't bother me as much? But right now, with all the photos and being in front of people, it's just really hard to ignore that unevenness.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your face is actually even and you're just perceiving it as uneven?\n\nPatient: Um, that I'm just perceiving it that way? I... I don't know. Like, is my brain just making it up? People were literally stepping around it because it looked so real, even though it was totally flat. \n\nBut the problem was, the artist insisted on using this special matte paint that took forever to dry, and he didn't finish until like, two hours before the guests arrived. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, if I could stop checking? I mean, maybe? But it's just... it's so hard to stop. Like, before I send out any event pictures for social media, or even just before I join a Zoom meeting with vendors, I'll just sit there checking my face in my phone camera. Over and over. But, um... sorry. What were we talking about? Oh, right, stopping the checking. Yeah, if I could just stop worrying about how it looks and checking my phone all the time, maybe things would improve. But right now, it just takes up so much of my time.

Um, if I could just stop thinking about it? I don't know... I really don't think I can. I mean, I want to, obviously. It's exhausting. But it's like it's just stuck in my head on a loop, you know? 

It's sort of like this one song that the DJ played at a wedding reception I coordinated last weekend. The couple had given him a very strict "do not play" list, and right at the top of it was the Macarena. They were adamant about it—no cheesy line dances, very elegant, very upscale vibe. But I guess the DJ's laptop crashed or something, and he had to switch to his backup drive, and suddenly, right in the middle of dinner service, the Macarena starts blasting through the speakers. 

I swear, my heart stopped. I sprinted across the ballroom in these awful heels—which, by the way, I only wore because the bride insisted the event staff wear silver shoes to match the decor, and they were giving me the worst blisters. Anyway, I get to the DJ booth, and he's just frantically clicking his mouse, sweating bullets, trying to get it to stop. But the system had totally frozen. So for a solid two minutes, everyone is just sitting there eating their filet mignon while this ridiculous song is playing. I had to literally crawl under the table and pull the main power cord to kill the audio. The bride was glaring at me from the head table, and I spent the rest of the night apologizing and offering them a partial refund on the entertainment package. It was a nightmare. And the worst part is, that stupid song has been stuck in my head ever since. I wake up, and it's playing in my brain. 

But, um... sorry, what was the question? Oh, stopping the thoughts about my face. Yeah, no, I don't think I can just turn it off, even if I want to. It just stays there.

People just do not pay attention to detail, and it drives me insane. \n\nBut yeah, with my face... people say they don't really see it, even though I can't stop looking at it in pictures.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that the unevenness of your face is actually there, even if other people don't notice it?\n\nPatient: Um, is it actually there? I mean... I don't really know. Like, obviously I see it, but I guess I can't say for sure if it's, like, objectively there the way I see it or not. I'm just not sure. \n\nHonestly, trying to figure out what's real and what's just me overthinking is basically my entire life right now. Like with the seating chart for the VIP dinner next week. I spent four hours last night staring at this massive spreadsheet, trying to figure out if putting the regional director next to the keynote speaker was a subtle power move or just a logistical necessity because of the dietary restrictions. The keynote is strictly vegan, but the regional director is doing keto, and the venue's kitchen is so small I had to group the special meals near the service doors so the waitstaff doesn't cause a traffic jam. And then I started second-guessing if the service doors are too loud and if it's going to ruin the ambiance during the speeches. I ended up emailing the venue coordinator at 2 AM asking for the decibel rating of their kitchen doors swinging shut. She probably thinks I'm completely unhinged. But if someone's giving a toast and all you hear is plates crashing in the background, who gets blamed? Me. It's always me. \n\nBut, um, anyway... about my face being uneven... yeah, I really don't know for sure. I just know what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face looks uneven when you're not looking at pictures of yourself?\n\nPatient: Um, when I'm not looking at pictures? I don't really know... I guess I'm not sure. It's hard to say. \n\nActually, speaking of pictures, you wouldn't believe the absolute nightmare I'm dealing with right now regarding the photo booth vendor for this weekend's corporate retreat. The vendor—who I specifically vetted, by the way—sent over the digital proofs for the printed photo strips yesterday, and they used the complete wrong hex code for the company's logo. It was supposed to be this very specific, cool-toned slate gray, and it printed out looking like this muddy, warm taupe color. I was furious. I mean, branding is literally everything for this client. If the CEO sees taupe on the photo strips, he's going to lose his mind. \n\nSo I had to drop everything and drive all the way out to their warehouse in the middle of rush hour traffic just to look at their printer settings in person. And it turns out they were just using cheap, third-party ink cartridges to cut costs! I had to threaten to pull their contract entirely and blast them on Yelp just to get them to recalibrate the machine and use the right ink. It is just so exhausting having to micromanage grown adults who can't even do the one job they were hired to do. \n\nBut, um, sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, my face without pictures. Yeah, I don't really have a good answer for that. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look uneven to you even if nobody else noticed it or commented on it?\n\nPatient: Um, if nobody else noticed it? I mean... I don't know. I really don't. It's so hard to say what I'd see if I wasn't constantly worrying about what other people are seeing, you know? \n\nWhich is exactly the issue I'm having with the floral arrangements for the gala on Saturday. The florist tried to tell me that nobody is going to notice if we swap out the white hydrangeas for white carnations to save on the budget. And I'm like, are you kidding me? Carnations? For a black-tie gala? It's not a high school prom! I don't care if the guests don't know the difference between a hydrangea and a carnation—I know the difference. And it completely changes the texture of the centerpieces. Hydrangeas are full and soft, and carnations just look so... cheap and jagged. \n\nI literally spent an hour on the phone with her yesterday, begging her to just find a wholesaler who could rush-deliver the hydrangeas. She kept saying, \"It's just white flowers, nobody is looking that closely.\" But that's the thing! It doesn't matter if they don't look closely, because the overall effect is ruined. If the centerpieces look cheap, the whole room feels cheap, and then the client thinks I don't know what I'm doing. I ended up having to pull from the audiovisual budget just to cover the difference in the floral costs, which means now I have to figure out how to light the stage with two fewer spotlights, and I am stressed out of my mind about it. \n\nBut, um... yeah, about my face... I just really don't know if I'd still see it the same way. I'm just not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look uneven even if a plastic surgeon or a medical professional told you that it's perfectly normal and there's nothing wrong with it?\n\nPatient: Um, if a doctor told me it was normal? I mean... I guess I'm not sure. Like, would I believe them? I don't know... it's hard to trust people when they tell you things are fine, you know? \n\nLike with the catering company for this upcoming tech launch. The head chef kept telling me, \"It's fine, it's fine, the appetizers will be ready on time,\" but I knew they wouldn't be. I just knew it. I kept looking at his prep schedule, and he had only allocated 45 minutes to assemble 300 miniature beef Wellingtons. I'm sorry, but unless you have an army of culinary students in the back, that is physically impossible. I told him he needed to start prep earlier, and he just brushed me off, saying, \"I'm a professional, I know what I'm doing.\" \n\nWell, guess what? We did a dry run yesterday, and he was 20 minutes late getting the first tray out. Twenty minutes! At a tech launch, 20 minutes is an eternity. People get bored, they start looking at their phones, and the whole energy of the room just dies. I had to sit down with him and literally map out a minute-by-minute timeline for his kitchen staff, down to when they take the puff pastry out of the fridge so it doesn't get too warm. He was so annoyed with me, but I don't care. If those Wellingtons aren't perfect, it's my reputation on the line, not his. \n\nBut, um... sorry. What were you asking? Oh, right, the plastic surgeon thing. Yeah, I don't really know if I'd see it differently or not. I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness you see in your face might not be as noticeable or present as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Um, not as noticeable? Ever since I got promoted and I have to be the one actually out on the floor managing these events, being in front of all these clients and vendors... Satin is so shiny and cheap-looking compared to silk. It completely ruins the elegant, understated vibe we're going for. I had to spend three hours on the phone this morning trying to track down a domestic supplier who can expedite a hundred yards of French silk ribbon, and they are charging me double the original price. I had to pull from the entertainment budget just to cover it, and now I'm worried the string quartet is going to be short a cellist. I mean... I guess I'm not really sure.
